Memo Artscape Newsletter MinnPost Timberwolves Newsletter Careers Festival About Us Open dropdown menu Inside MinnPost Open Search Search for: Search Topics State Government Metro Greater Minnesota Elections Washington Mental Health &#038; Addiction Photography Sports Documenters Fact Briefs Posted in Politics & Policy Here’s who’s running for the Minnesota Legislature by Tom Nehil and Greta Kaul 06/01/2016 06/03/2016 Share this: Email a link to a friend (Opens in new window) Email Share on Facebook (Opens in new window) Facebook Share on Bluesky (Opens in new window) Bluesky Share on X (Opens in new window) X Print (Opens in new window) Print [raw shortcodes=1] Ready or not, the race to determine who controls the Minnesota Legislature is on. Tuesday was the deadline for candidates to file to run for the Minnesota Senate and House of Representatives. In total, 434 candidates filed to run for the 201 seats in play in the 2016 election. Not all contests are created equal, of course. There are five incumbents — DFL Sen. Ron Latz; DFL Reps. Michael Nelson and Susan Allen; and GOP Reps. Paul Anderson and Dean Urdahl — who can take the summer off from campaigning: nobody filed to run against them. But most races attracted a bit more attention, including 11 races that will require a Republican primary and 17 that will have a DFL primary. Use the tool below to explore the 2016 legislative election. For each race, we list the candidates who filed and their party affiliations, as well as the results of the district’s most recent regular election (some districts may have had subsequent special elections). You can use the “Show only” links to look at certain kinds of races, or search for a candidate by name. You can also click multiple filters in order to combine them; for example: if you want to see only House races with Republican primaries, click the "House" and "R Primary" links. Clicking "Show All" will clear the filters. If you have questions, email feedback@minnpost.com . — The Editors Here’s who’s running for the Minnesota Legislature by Tom Nehil and Greta Kaul, MinnPost June 1, 2016 &lt;h1&gt;Here’s who’s running for the Minnesota Legislature&lt;/h1&gt; &lt;p class="byline"&gt;by Tom Nehil and Greta Kaul, MinnPost &lt;br /&gt;June 1, 2016&lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt;[raw shortcodes=1]&lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; body .node-unpublished {background-repeat: no-repeat;}&lt;br /&gt; .hidden {display: none;}&lt;br /&gt; .filter-links li a {padding:5px; font-size: .8em;}&lt;br /&gt; .filtering, .filtering a {color: #fff!important; background-color:#0793AB; border-radius: 5px;}&lt;br /&gt; .mp .election {padding: 1em; border-bottom: 1px solid #ccc;}&lt;br /&gt; .mp .map-link {margin: -1em 0 1em;}&lt;br /&gt; .mp #total-contests {padding-bottom: 1em; border-bottom: 1px solid #ccc;}&lt;br /&gt; .search-box {width: 100%; margin: 0 auto 1em; text-align: center}&lt;br /&gt; .mp .form-input-group {width:80%; margin: 0 auto;}&lt;br /&gt; #text_search {width: 100%;}&lt;br /&gt; #total-contests {text-align: center;}&lt;/p&gt; &lt;div class="mp"&gt; &lt;div class="text-block large"&gt; &lt;p&gt;Ready or not, the race to determine who controls the Minnesota Legislature is on. Tuesday was the deadline for candidates to file to run for the Minnesota Senate and House of Representatives. In total, 434 candidates filed to run for the 201 seats in play in the 2016 election.&lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt;Not all contests are created equal, of course. There are five incumbents — DFL Sen. Ron Latz; DFL Reps. Michael Nelson and Susan Allen; and GOP Reps. Paul Anderson and Dean Urdahl — who can take the summer off from campaigning: nobody filed to run against them. But most races attracted a bit more attention, including 11 races that will require a Republican primary and 17 that will have a DFL primary.&lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt;Use the tool below to explore the 2016 legislative election. For each race, we list the candidates who filed and their party affiliations, as well as the results of the district’s most recent regular election (some districts may have had subsequent special elections). You can use the “Show only” links to look at certain kinds of races, or search for a candidate by name. You can also click multiple filters in order to combine them; for example: if you want to see only House races with Republican primaries, click the "House" and "R Primary" links. Clicking "Show All" will clear the filters.&lt;/p&gt; &lt;/p&gt;&lt;/div&gt; &lt;/div&gt; &lt;p&gt;[/raw][raw shortcodes=1]&lt;/p&gt; &lt;div class="mp"&gt; &lt;div class="filter-links navigation horizontal"&gt;
